## 上一轮交接 (task_context)

### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
- 本项目无 GPU 实验，纯论文写作项目——所有实验数据来自 Wang et al. (2025) 预训练 model zoo，不需要训练
- 论文 PDF 路径：`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/docs/paper/main.pdf`
- EMNLP 2026 ARR DDL 是 **2026-05-25（今天）**，主文严格 8 页限制（不含 References/Appendix/Limitations）
- Abstract ≤ 200 words（当前 ~154 词，合规）
- 审稿已进入 Review 阶段（Group C: review_chat_id `oc_b542b52ff24f138c42225a1d4b456e7e`），Research Director 不参与格式/投稿材料准备

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
- 无远程服务器，所有工作在本地 workspace 完成
- 论文源码：`projects/entrainment_gated_ft/docs/paper/`（main.tex + sections/*.tex）
- 图表生成脚本：`projects/entrainment_gated_ft/figures/paper/gen_fig*.py`
- 统一配色 palette：`figures/paper/palette.py`（BLUE=#4E79A7, CORAL=#C56C6C, TEAL=#59A572, AMBER=#E49444, GRAY=#999999, DARK=#333333）
- Python：`python3`（系统 Python，matplotlib 已通过 `--break-system-packages` 安装）
- 无活跃进程 / 无 GPU / 无 tmux / 无 cron

### 3. 📊 当前状态
**刚完成**：
- R21 审稿报告 Critical/Major 文本修复全部完成：
  - 全文 "saturates" → "appears to stabilize"（Abstract/Intro/Conclusion/Contributions/§4.2 标题/Figure 1 caption/Appendix）

  - "co-varies with model capacity" → "tracks model capacity"

  - Permutation test §3.5: "conservative" → "may be anti-conservative" + 2.5× margin 论证
  - Yang & Wang (2026) 引用已验证真实（arXiv:2602.11898）
- 周俊伟排版修改全部完成：
  - Figure 1A ".024" 标签移到数据点右上方，不再与 Y 轴重叠
  - Figure 4 (alpha_routing) 前移到第 5 页（左右两栏各一图）
  - Figure 5 (task_specificity) 移到第 7 页右栏
  - 编译 PASS，21 页（8 页主文 + References + Appendix），无 critical warnings
- review_issues.yaml 已更新：R5-W2/R16-N1/R20-W4 的 R21 action items 标为 in_progress

**Registry**：无 RUNNING/PREPARING 实验（纯写作项目）
**等待中**：无 Worker 回调 / 无 Cron

### 4. 📋 待办
- 用户确认提交 → 可能需要最终 `submit_paper_to_review` 提交 Group C 做最后一轮格式检查
- Paper Agent PaperBanana Figure 2 重新生成任务此前已派给 Main Agent，但未收到结果回调——如果收到，需要替换 `figures/fig_method_overview.pdf` 并重新编译
- 如果 Review Director (Group C) 回传新审稿报告 → 按 `review-report-action` skill 处理（Holistic Pass + Action Matrix）
- R21 review_issues.yaml 中仍有多个 `open` 状态 action items（R6-W3 Table 2 n=3 标注、R16-W3 'reliable'→'moderate'、R16-W5 补 selective prediction 文献、R20-W3 Limitations 补 greedy decoding）——这些是 Minor/Info 级别，DDL 当天不一定来得及，但如果用户要求可以快速修复

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
- **核心结论**：hybrid linear recurrent architectures 在 aggregate benchmarks 上看似等价，但 per-example 层面存在 structured predictive multiplicity（α=0.14-0.30），由 task type 而非 attention ratio 决定；per-example overlap 在 4% attention 即稳定（J≈0.10）
- **投稿定位**：EMNLP 2026 Findings（R21 Overall 3.0），empirical contribution paper
- **7 轮反复的核心顽疾 (R5-W2)**：n=5 Spearman 检验力近乎为零，claim-evidence 脱节。本轮已全文替换为 descriptive language，但结构性问题无法在不增加数据点的情况下根本解决——wont_fix 级别
- **易踩的坑**：
  - "saturates" 一词在全文中有 12+ 处，已全部替换但 author_response_r7.tex 等历史文件中仍有——这些是旧 rebuttal 不需要改
  - Figure 定位靠 LaTeX float 机制，移动 figure 定义位置会产生连锁效应——每次改完必须编译+视觉检查全部 8 页
  - `paper_compile_and_check` 的 max_pages 参数计算的是 PDF 总页数（含 appendix），不是主文页数——检查 8 页限制要看 PDF 预览而非工具返回的 pages 字段
  - `submit_paper_to_review` 需要绝对路径 + 最近 2h 内有 visual check 记录

[Reviewer 回复]:
[severity: info] DDL 日最终审查报告 — 提交前检查通过

## 三轮遗留问题全部解决

1. **"attention dose"** — 已清除，全文 0 匹配（此前遗留 6 处，跨 R19-R21 三轮未修）。✅
2. **"saturates"** — 正文 .tex 文件中已全部清除（仅残留在 author_response 和 figure planning JSON 中，不影响提交）。✅
3. **Yang & Wang (2026) arXiv:2602.11898** — **WebSearch 验证为真实引用**。作者（Eddie Yang, Purdue; Dashun Wang, Northwestern）、标题（"Benchmark Illusion: Disagreement among LLMs and Its Scientific Consequences"）、arXiv ID、2026年2月发表日期均与 bib 条目完全吻合。三轮幻觉引用风险正式解除。✅

## EMNLP 2026 格式合规

- **页数**: 主文（含 Conclusion）在第 8 页内结束，Limitations 从 p8 底部开始延伸到 p9——Limitations 不计入 8 页限制，合规 ✅
- **Abstract**: ~170 词，低于 200 词限制 ✅
- **Limitations**: 在正文内（main.tex L52-60），不在 Appendix，含 4 个段落（Single training runs / Format confound / Coverage / Pre-training only）✅
- **ARR checklist**: 不在 PDF 中（由用户在 OpenReview form 填写）✅

## check_figures_and_layout 工具报告澄清

工具报告 2 个 critical（`\ref{app:seed_calibration}` 和 `\ref{app:normalized_es}` 无匹配 label）——经核实为**误报**，两个 label 均存在于 appendix.tex（L728 和 L215），.aux 文件确认解析正确。Ethics Statement 警告可忽略（EMNLP 2026 规则明确为可选）。

## GPU 状态

96	
97	Each architecture's per-example response to attention is largely idiosyncratic. The observed $\bar{J}$ reaches only 6--7\% of the achievable range above chance ($J_{\max} \approx 0.78$--$0.92$), ruling out ceiling constraints (\S\ref{ssec:per_example}). Readers can verify $\rho$ directly from Table~\ref{tab:convergence}; with $n = 3$ architectures, all bootstrap CIs are heuristic indicators of direction rather than precise coverage.
98	
99	This pattern generalizes beyond LAMBADA (Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling}c): on all four main benchmarks, mean pairwise Jaccard shows no significant trend with attention ratio (all $\rho \leq 0.80$, $p \geq 0.333$), with values uniformly between 0.08 and 0.11.
100

78	
79	\subsection{Per-Example Overlap Appears Stable from Minimal Attention}\label{ssec:decoupling}
80	
81	Does adding more attention change \emph{which} examples each architecture affects? Pairwise Jaccard appears to stabilize at the first attention level (4\%; $\bar{J} \approx 0.095$) and remains flat across all higher ratios (Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling}b), suggesting that minimal attention may suffice to establish the per-example divergence pattern, though the small number of ratios ($n = 5$) limits statistical confirmation. As background, LAMBADA's aggregate cross-architecture $\sigma$ shows a non-significant decreasing trend ($\rho = -1.0$, Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$; excluding GDN: $\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$; MMLU reverses to $\rho = +0.6$ when excluding GDN; observed at 1.3B, absent at 340M; Table~\ref{tab:convergence}, Figure~\ref{fig:task_specificity}). No other benchmark shows a comparable aggregate pattern ($n = 5$; negligible power for $|\rho| < 1.0$).
82	
83	\begin{figure*}[t]
84	\centering
85	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/jaccard_heatmap_5arch.pdf}
86	\caption{Pairwise Jaccard heatmap across five architectures (340M, h3-1), where helped (hurt) examples are those correct only with (without) attention. Upper triangle: $J_{\text{helped}}$; lower triangle: $J_{\text{hurt}}$. All values $\leq 0.14$, all permutation tests $p \leq .002$. HellaSwag and PIQA results in Appendix~\ref{app:extended_arch}.}
87	\label{fig:jaccard_heatmap_5arch}
88	\end{figure*}
89	
90	Each architecture's per-example response to attention is largely idiosyncratic. The observed $\bar{J}$ reaches only 6--7\% of the achievable range above chance ($J_{\max} \approx 0.78$--$0.92$), ruling out ceiling constraints (\S\ref{ssec:per_example}). 83	\caption{Spearman $\rho$ between attention ratio and cross-architecture $\sigma$ for each benchmark at 1.3B scale. LAMBADA ($\rho = -1.0$; BCa 95\% CI $[-1.0,\,-0.7]$, heuristic) is the only benchmark with a negative correlation whose confidence interval excludes zero. Benchmarks are grouped by task category. Bootstrap CI is heuristic given $n = 3$ architectures.}
84	\label{fig:task_specificity}
85	\end{figure}
86	
87	\subsection{Per-Example Overlap Appears Stable from Minimal Attention}\label{ssec:decoupling}
88	
89	Does adding more attention change \emph{which} examples each architecture affects? Pairwise Jaccard appears to stabilize at the first attention level (4\%; $\bar{J} \approx 0.095$) and remains flat across all higher ratios (Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling}b), suggesting that minimal attention may suffice to establish the per-example divergence pattern, though the small number of ratios ($n = 5$) limits statistical confirmation. As background, LAMBADA's aggregate cross-architecture $\sigma$ shows a non-significant decreasing trend ($\rho = -1.0$, Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$; excluding GDN: $\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$; MMLU reverses to $\rho = +0.6$ when excluding GDN; observed at 1.3B, absent at 340M; Table~\ref{tab:convergence}, Figure~\ref{fig:task_specificity}). No other benchmark shows a comparable aggregate pattern ($n = 5$; negligible power for $|\rho| < 1.0$).
90	
91	\begin{figure*}[t]
92	\centering
93	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/jaccard_heatmap_5arch.pdf}

  "old_string": "Does adding more attention change \\emph{which} examples each architecture affects? Pairwise Jaccard appears to stabilize at the first attention level (4\\%; $\\bar{J} \\approx 0.095$) and remains flat across all higher ratios (Figure~\\ref{fig:decoupling}b), suggesting that minimal attention may suffice to establish the per-example divergence pattern, though the small number of ratios ($n = 5$) limits statistical confirmation.\n%\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_task_specificity.pdf}\n\\caption{Spearman $\\rho$ between attention ratio and cross-architecture $\\sigma$ for each benchmark at 1.3B scale. LAMBADA ($\\rho = -1.0$; BCa 95\\% CI $[-1.0,\\,-0.7]$, heuristic) is the only benchmark with a negative correlation whose confidence interval excludes zero. Benchmarks are grouped by task category. Bootstrap CI is heuristic given $n = 3$ architectures.}\n\\label{fig:task_specificity}\n\\end{figure}\n%\nAs background, LAMBADA's aggregate cross-architecture $\\sigma$ shows a non-significant decreasing trend ($\\rho = -1.0$, Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$; excluding GDN: $\\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$; MMLU reverses to $\\rho = +0.6$ when excluding GDN; observed at 1.3B, absent at 340M; Table~\\ref{tab:convergence}, Figure~\\ref{fig:task_specificity}). No other benchmark shows a comparable aggregate pattern ($n = 5$; negligible power for $|\\rho| < 1.0$).",

As background, LAMBADA's aggregate cross-architecture $\\sigma$ shows a non-significant decreasing trend ($\\rho = -1.0$, Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$; excluding GDN: $\\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$; MMLU reverses to $\\rho = +0.6$ when excluding GDN; observed at 1.3B, absent at 340M; Table~\\ref{tab:convergence}, Figure~\\ref{fig:task_specificity}).\n%\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_task_specificity.pdf}\n\\caption{Spearman $\\rho$ between attention ratio and cross-architecture $\\sigma$ for each benchmark at 1.3B scale. LAMBADA ($\\rho = -1.0$; BCa 95\\% CI $[-1.0,\\,-0.7]$, heuristic) is the only benchmark with a negative correlation whose confidence interval excludes zero. Benchmarks are grouped by task category. 82	Does adding more attention change \emph{which} examples each architecture affects? Pairwise Jaccard appears to stabilize at the first attention level (4\%; $\bar{J} \approx 0.095$) and remains flat across all higher ratios (Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling}b), suggesting that minimal attention may suffice to establish the per-example divergence pattern, though the small number of ratios ($n = 5$) limits statistical confirmation. As background, LAMBADA's aggregate cross-architecture $\sigma$ shows a non-significant decreasing trend ($\rho = -1.0$, Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$; excluding GDN: $\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$; MMLU reverses to $\rho = +0.6$ when excluding GDN; observed at 1.3B, absent at 340M; Table~\ref{tab:convergence}, Figure~\ref{fig:task_specificity}).

38	\begin{abstract}
39	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\approx 0.10$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. 请对当前论文做一次数据正确性与一致性自检。AI 写作另一个高频问题：图表里的数字、正文里引用的数字、Abstract/Conclusion 里的数字三者经常对不上，或者跟实验日志里的真实结果有出入。

自检要求：

1. 图表正确性

   - 表头、列名、单位、有效位数是否正确？
   - 图的坐标轴标签、图例、caption 描述与图内容是否一致？

2. 数字一致性（critical）
   - Abstract 里的关键数字（main result、improvement %、scale）

   - Conclusion 里复述的数字

   - 如果做过 cherry-pick（选了最好的 seed），是否在文中说明了 reporting protocol（best of N / mean ± std / median）？

1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures---including GatedDeltaNet (GDN), RetNet, HGRN2, Gated Linear Attention (GLA), and DeltaNet---achieve aggregate benchmark scores within 1--2 points of each other at matched parameter budgets, yet they can differ on up to 90\% of the individual examples they affect. This per-example divergence is invisible in aggregate metrics: architectures that score similarly reach that score by gaining and losing on different inputs, and the choice of backbone carries hidden consequences for which specific examples a deployed system handles correctly.

1	\section{Method}\label{sec:method}
2	
3	We describe the model configurations, evaluation benchmarks, and analytical framework used to measure how attention affects cross-architecture agreement at both aggregate and per-example levels.

24	\subsection{Model Zoo}\label{ssec:model_zoo}

26	We use the pretrained model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid} (Table~\ref{tab:model_zoo}), which provides controlled comparisons: GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet}, RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet}, and HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} at five attention ratios and two scales. The GDN 1.3B h3-1 configuration uses 16 attention heads vs.\ 32 for RetNet and HGRN2 (\S\ref{ssec:robustness}). We adopt the h$k$-1 notation: h3-1 = 25\% attention, h6-1 = 14.3\%, h12-1 = 7.7\%, h24-1 = 4\%. Within each scale, all models share identical training data, tokenizer, and hyperparameters, isolating architecture and attention configuration. The zoo also includes a Transformer baseline at both scales.
27

_source line 201 · 2026-05-25T08:32:06.325499+00:00_

1	\vspace{-2pt}
2	\section{Conclusion}
3	
4	We have shown that hybrid linear recurrent architectures which appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks diverge substantially at the per-example level. This multiplicity varies by task type ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\% attention (pairwise Jaccard $\leq 0.10$); two interpretations---architecture-specific fingerprints or rapid convergence toward Transformer-like behavior---remain open (Appendix~\ref{app:transformer_entrainment}). Confidence margins partially predict which examples are affected (AUC 0.70--0.74), with rescue thresholds architecture-invariant but disruption thresholds varying 47\%.
5	
6	At the 340M--1.3B scales studied here, per-example audit before architecture substitution is advisable when multiplicity is substantial---aggregate equivalence does not guarantee behavioral equivalence. Low cross-architecture Jaccard creates complementarity ($+7$--$14$\,pp oracle gain), though preliminary routing experiments show exploitation remains task-dependent ($+5.2$\,pp on LAMBADA; $-0.49$\,pp on PIQA).
7

1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Hybrid Recurrent Architectures.}
4	The design space for combining attention with recurrence spans layer-level interleaving \citep[Jamba;][]{lieber2024jamba}, local attention pairing \citep[Griffin, RecurrentGemma;][]{de2024griffin,botev2024recurrentgemma}, matrix-valued recurrence \citep[RWKV-6;][]{peng2024eagle}, within-head mixing \citep[Hymba;][]{dong2024hymba}, and unbounded-context hybrid SSMs \citep[Samba;][]{ren2024samba}. H3 \citep{fu2023hungry} and Hyena \citep{poli2023hyena} establish early design points in the hybrid space with gated convolutions and subquadratic operators. Mamba-2 \citep{dao2024mamba2} establishes a duality between structured state spaces and attention; Mamba-3 \citep{lahoti2026mamba3} extends the design further, while \citet{ali2025hidden} reveal implicit attention within Mamba. Comparative evaluations confirm that hybrids outperform pure Transformers and SSMs at scale \citep{waleffe2024empirical}, and that even one attention layer enables content-based routing \citep{basu2026routing}. Recent theoretical work establishes a provable expressiveness hierarchy between hybrid and pure linear attention \citep{ye2026provable}, providing formal grounding for the architectural differences we characterize at the per-example level. \citet{wang2025hybrid} provide the controlled model zoo we use, holding training conditions constant while varying architecture and attention ratio.
5	
6	\paragraph{Linear Recurrent Models.}
7	Our three recurrent backbones represent distinct gating strategies within the linear recurrence family: RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet} uses multi-scale exponential decay, HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} applies gated state expansion, and GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet} combines gated updates with delta rules. These descend from S4 \citep{gu2022s4} and Mamba \citep{gu2024mamba}, which introduced structured state spaces and input-dependent selection, respectively, and connect to linear a
…[truncated 1516 chars]

1	% Appendix sections for the paper.
2	
3	\section{Confirmatory and Exploratory Analyses}\label{app:benchmark_details}
4	
5	The task taxonomy used in \S\ref{ssec:task_taxonomy} was defined prior to running any convergence analyses. Each benchmark was assigned to exactly one of four categories based on the primary cognitive demand of the task:
6	
7	\begin{itemize}
8	    \item \textbf{Retrieval}: tasks requiring prediction from long-range context without multi-step reasoning (LAMBADA).
9	    \item \textbf{Comprehension}: reading comprehension with explicit passage support (BoolQ).
10	    \item \textbf{Reasoning}: tasks requiring commonsense inference or multi-step reasoning (HellaSwag, ARC-Challenge, WinoGrande, PIQA).
11	    \item \textbf{Mixed}: tasks combining retrieval, comprehension, and reasoning demands (ARC-Easy, MMLU, SciQ, XStoryCloze).
12	\end{itemize}
13	
14	\paragraph{Confirmatory benchmark set.} Six benchmarks were designated as confirmatory for Bonferroni correction: LAMBADA, HellaSwag, ARC-Challenge, WinoGrande, ARC-Easy, and MMLU. These were selected as representative of retrieval, reasoning, and mixed categories before any convergence analysis was performed. The taxonomy and benchmark selection were fixed before analysis as an informal pre-analysis plan; we describe this as a confirmatory/exploratory split rather than a formal pre-registration, since no external timestamp exists. The main text applies the more conservative all-10-benchmark correction (Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .017 \times 10 = .170$). \textbf{Sensitivity:} correcting across only the six confirmatory benchmarks yields adjusted $p = .017 \times 6 = .100$, still above conventional significance.
15	
16	\paragraph{One-sided test.} If one adopts the prior that attention should reduce cross-architecture variance, the one-sided permutation $p$-value for LAMBADA is $1/120 \approx 0.0083$. Under 10-benchmark Bonferroni correction, this yields an adjusted $p = 0.083$, above the conventional thre

[飞书用户 用户]: 🔐 论文自检任务：匿名仓库重整与链接位置规范化

这条消息既包含仓库本身的整理，也包含论文里链接位置的统一规范。请逐条对照。

---

Part 1 — 你的匿名仓库

你这个项目分配到的匿名仓库地址是：

https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026-2/entrainment_gated_ft

请按以下步骤检查：

1. 验证密钥有效性：从项目 secret vault / config/projects/{project}.yaml 拿到对应的 GitHub 部署密钥（deploy key 或 PAT），用它执行一次 git push 或 dry-run（git push --dry-run）确认密钥仍然有效。如果已失效，立刻通过审批流找用户申请新密钥。

2. 仓库内容自检：
   - 匿名性：commit author / committer 字段没有真实姓名或邮箱（应该是 anonymous@anonymous.invalid 之类的占位）；README / LICENSE / 代码注释 / requirements.txt 里没有真名、机构名、师生关系暗示
   - 完整性：README.md 写清楚怎么跑（环境、依赖、入口脚本、数据获取）；核心代码、训练/评测脚本、关键 config 都在；requirements.txt / environment.yml 能 reproduce
   - 干净度：没有 .DS_Store、__pycache__、*.pyc、.env、wandb/、大量临时实验日志；没有未引用的死代码堆积
   - 数据可获取：如果论文用了自定义数据集，README 写明数据放在哪儿（仓库内 / 公开链接 / 申请方式）

3. 重新推送一遍：自检完后，用有效密钥把整理过的代码 force-push 到匿名仓库（覆盖旧的）。

---

Part 2 — 论文里链接的统一标准

ACL/EMNLP 社区惯例 + 我们的统一规范如下：

位置：第一页脚注（footnote）。挂在 Abstract 末尾（最理想）或 Introduction 第一段提到 contribution 的句子。

禁止：放在 Appendix、放在 References、藏在论文末尾、用 Google Drive / Dropbox / 个人网盘。

标准 LaTeX 格式（请严格采用以下措辞之一）：

挂 Abstract 末尾：
... (Abstract 最后一句) We release our code and data at\footnote{\url{https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026-2/entrainment_gated_ft}}.

或挂 Introduction 第一段 contribution：
Our contributions are: (1) ..., (2) ..., (3) ... . Code and data are available at\footnote{\url{https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026-2/entrainment_gated_ft}}.

只放一处脚注，不要 abstract 一遍、intro 又一遍、appendix 再一遍。

---

完成后请汇报：

(a) 密钥是否有效？如失效，是否已申请补发？
(b) 仓库重新推送完成时间戳是？
(c) 论文里链接放在第几页 / 哪个章节 / 用了上面哪个模板？
(d) 链接的匿名性确认过了吗（commit author / README 等没有暴露身份）？

_source line 317 · 2026-05-25T08:45:42.861521+00:00_

1	\vspace{-2pt}
2	\section{Conclusion}
3	
4	We have shown that hybrid linear recurrent architectures which appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks diverge substantially at the per-example level. This multiplicity varies by task type ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\% attention (pairwise Jaccard $\leq 0.10$); two interpretations---architecture-specific fingerprints or rapid convergence toward Transformer-like behavior---remain open (Appendix~\ref{app:transformer_entrainment}). Confidence margins partially predict which examples are affected (AUC 0.70--0.74), with rescue thresholds architecture-invariant but disruption thresholds varying 47\%.
5	
6	At the 340M--1.3B scales studied here, per-example audit before architecture substitution is advisable when multiplicity is substantial---aggregate equivalence does not guarantee behavioral equivalence. Low cross-architecture Jaccard creates complementarity ($+7$--$14$\,pp oracle gain), though preliminary routing experiments show exploitation remains task-dependent ($+5.3$\,pp on LAMBADA; $-0.6$\,pp on PIQA).
7

30	\title{Aggregate Equivalence $\neq$ Behavioral Equivalence: Per-Example Multiplicity in Hybrid Linear Recurrent Models}
31	
32	\author{Anonymous}
33	
34	\begin{document}
35	\maketitle
36	
37	\begin{abstract}
38	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\approx 0.10$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\% attention and remains flat across higher ratios, suggesting that minimal attention may establish which examples each archi

79	\subsection{Per-Example Overlap Appears Stable from Minimal Attention}\label{ssec:decoupling}
80	
81	Does adding more attention change \emph{which} examples each architecture affects? Pairwise Jaccard appears to stabilize at the first attention level (4\%; $\bar{J} \approx 0.095$) and remains flat across all higher ratios (Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling}b), suggesting that minimal attention may suffice to establish the per-example divergence pattern, though the small number of ratios ($n = 5$) limits statistical confirmation. As background, LAMBADA's aggregate cross-architecture $\sigma$ shows a non-significant decreasing trend ($\rho = -1.0$, Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$; excluding GDN: $\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$; MMLU reverses to $\rho = +0.6$ when excluding GDN; observed at 1.3B, absent at 340M; Table~\ref{tab:convergence}, Figure~\ref{fig:task_specificity}). No other benchmark shows a comparable aggregate pattern ($n = 5$; negligible power for $|\rho| < 1.0$).
82	
83	\newpage
84	
85	\begin{figure}[t]
86	\centering
87	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_task_specificity.pdf}
88	\caption{Spearman $\rho$ between attention ratio and cross-architecture $\sigma$ for each benchmark at 1.3B scale. LAMBADA ($\rho = -1.0$; BCa 95\% CI $[-1.0,\,-0.7]$, heuristic) is the only benchmark with a negative correlation whose confidence interval excludes zero. Benchmarks are grouped by task category. Bootstrap CI is heuristic given $n = 3$ architectures.}
89	\label{fig:task_specificity}
90	\end{figure}

### User

_source line 353 · 2026-05-25T09:01:24.321566+00:00_

[Reviewer 回复]:
[severity: critical] 主文页数溢出——Conclusion 溢出到第 9 页，违反 EMNLP 2026 八页硬限

## 现状确认

PDF 视觉审查确认：experiments.tex:83 的 `\newpage` 导致 p7 左栏底部留白约 1/4 栏，后续内容整体下移，Conclusion 第二段（"At the 340M–1.3B scales studied here..."）溢出到 p9 顶部。主文（Abstract→Conclusion）占 9 页，超限 1 页。

## 8 页限制严格程度

ARR/EMNLP 的 8 页限制是 **desk reject 级硬约束**。提交系统和 AC 均会检查。溢出哪怕几行都可能被退回，不存在"容忍几行"的惯例。**必须在提交前修复。**

## 方案评估

**方案 1（推荐）：去掉 `\newpage`** 
- 最安全、最干净。Table 3 / Figure 5 的同页对齐是 nice-to-have，不值得冒 desk reject 风险
- LaTeX float 算法会自动重排，可能 Table 3 / Figure 5 不再在同一页，但不影响论文可读性
- 去掉后如果 float 位置不理想，可用 `[t]` / `[!t]` placement hint 微调，这是标准做法

**方案 2（次选）：保留 `\newpage` + 压缩正文 ~0.2 页**
- 风险较高：难以精确控制压缩量，可能需要删减有价值的分析文本
- 压缩方向：§4.2 的 "two interpretations" 讨论段或 §4.3 Robustness 可压缩 3-5 行；Conclusion 第二段可精简
- 但 DDL 日做内容压缩容易引入新问题

**方案 3（不推荐）：LaTeX 排版技巧**
- `\vspace` 负值、调 `\floatsep` / `\textfloatsep` 等——小幅调整（-2pt 级）可接受，但大幅压缩（-10pt+）会被审稿人视为格式操纵
- EMNLP/ARR 明确禁止修改 margins / text size / baselineskip
- 脆弱且不可预测，DDL 日不适合依赖

## 建议

**立即执行方案 1**：删除 experiments.tex:83 的 `\newpage`，重新编译，验证主文 ≤ 8 页。如果 float 位置不理想，仅用 `[t]` / `[!t]` 微调。DDL 日首要保证合规，排版美观是次要的。

13-
14-[Omitted long context line]
15-
16:\paragraph{One-sided test.} If one adopts the prior that attention should reduce cross-architecture variance, the one-sided permutation $p$-value for LAMBADA is $1/120 \approx 0.0083$. Under 10-benchmark Bonferroni correction, this yields an adjusted $p = 0.083$, above the conventional threshold. We report two-sided tests throughout the main text as the more conservative choice; the one-sided result is noted here for completeness.
17-
18:\paragraph{Exploratory benchmarks.} Four additional benchmarks (BoolQ, PIQA, SciQ, XStoryCloze) were added as exploratory tests. All benchmarks share the same 10-benchmark correction (adjusted threshold $\alpha / 10 = 0.005$) and the four exploratory benchmarks are labeled as such throughout the paper. The all-10 Bonferroni correction reported in the main text is the only correction used for inference; the exploratory 6-benchmark subset correction in this appendix is provided for transparency.
19-
20:\paragraph{Supplementary benchmark.} COPA ($n = 100$) is treated as supplementary due to its small sample size. It is excluded from both correction families and reported separately with the $\dagger$ annotation in Table~\ref{tab:convergence}.
21-
22-\section{Convergence Detail}\label{app:convergence_detail}
23-
--

42-[Omitted long context line]
43-
44:The BCa (bias-corrected and accelerated) correction degenerates at the $\rho = -1.0$ boundary because the acceleration parameter requires finite jackknife variance, which collapses when the point estimate is at the distributional limit. We therefore report percentile confidence intervals instead. From the 88.75\% informative resamples, the 95\% percentile CI is $[-1.0,\,-0.7]$, which excludes zero.
45-

### Tool result

_source line 389 · 2026-05-25T09:06:13.118535+00:00_

25	
26	Our central finding is that architectural variation produces structured predictive multiplicity whose magnitude depends on task type, not attention ratio. The ambiguity $\alpha$ \citep{marx2020predictive} ranges from 0.14 (PIQA) to 0.30 (LAMBADA), varies $2\times$ across task types but only 0.03 within each benchmark across attention ratios; pairwise Jaccard remains uniformly low ($\leq 0.10$, all $p < .01$ across all ten five-architecture pairs). A supporting observation is that per-example overlap appears to stabilize at the first attention level (4\%) and remains flat at higher ratios---even on LAMBADA, where aggregate variance shows a non-significant decreasing trend ($\rho = -1.0$, Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$; at 1.3B only), per-example Jaccard does not change (\S\ref{ssec:decoupling}).
27	
28	Our contributions are as follows:
29	\begin{itemize}
30	    \item Architectural variation in hybrid recurrent models---controlled within a single model zoo sharing training data and hyperparameters---produces structured predictive multiplicity whose magnitude varies by task type (or evaluation format) rather than attention ratio (\S\ref{ssec:per_example}). Five-architecture validation confirms the pattern (\S\ref{ssec:robustness}; Appendix~\ref{app:extended_arch}).
31	    \item Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at the first attention level (4\%) across all four benchmarks: minimal attention may establish which examples each architecture affects, with no observed homogenization at higher ratios ($n = 5$ ratios; \S\ref{ssec:decoupling}).
32	    \item Pure-model confidence margins partially predict which examples attention will affect (AUC 0.70--0.74), with rescue thresholds architecture-invariant but disruption thresholds varying 47\% across architectures---a second dimension of multiplicity beyond per-example disagreement (\S\ref{ssec:per_example}; Appendix~\ref{app:prediction_framework}).
33	\end{itemize}
34

779	All pairwise Jaccard values remain $\leq 0.14$, consistent with the three-architecture results at 1.3B (Table~\ref{tab:per_example}). The seven new pairs involving GLA and DeltaNet show no systematic difference from the original three pairs (Mann-Whitney $p > .3$ for both helped and hurt), indicating that the low-overlap pattern is not specific to the original architecture triple. A sixth architecture (HGRN; \citealp{qin2023hierarchically}) is also available in the zoo but excluded from the main analysis: its substantially lower baseline accuracy on LAMBADA (0.263 vs.\ 0.32--0.38 for the other five) mechanically inflates helped-set sizes and Jaccard values. All 15 six-architecture permutation tests nonetheless remain significant ($z \geq 2.9$, $p \leq .006$). The same pattern holds on HellaSwag ($N = 10{,}042$) and PIQA ($N = 1{,}838$): all 20 additional pairwise tests yield $p < .01$ with mean $J_{\text{helped}} \leq 0.10$. _source line 409 · 2026-05-25T09:07:52.465484+00:00_

[飞书用户 用户]: 📑 论文自检任务：附录内容引用完整性检查

请检查论文附录（Appendix）的所有内容是否在正文里都有准确、合理的引用。

AI 写作的高频问题：附录里塞了一堆补充材料（额外实验、超参表、prompt 模板、proof、failure case），但正文从头到尾没有一句话指向它们。这种"孤儿附录内容"对 reviewer 来说等于不存在 —— 既浪费篇幅，也让附录显得拼凑。

ACL 政策也明确：附录是 supplementary 性质的延伸，必须由正文驱动读者去看。完全没被引用的附录章节会被视为低质量补充材料。

---

自检要求：

1. 逐项盘点附录
   把附录里的每一个 section / subsection / table / figure / algorithm / proof / prompt 都列出来。常见类型：
   - Appendix A: Implementation details / Training details
   - Appendix B: Additional results / Ablations
   - Appendix C: Hyperparameter search
   - Appendix D: Prompt templates
   - Appendix E: Proofs
   - Appendix F: Failure cases / Error analysis / Qualitative examples
   - Appendix G: Dataset construction details

2. 逐项验证引用
   对每一项，在正文里搜：是否有至少一句话指向它？引用形式应该是：
   - "... (see Appendix A for full hyperparameters)"
   - "... we defer the proof to Appendix B"
   - "... Table 5 in Appendix C reports the breakdown"
   - "... Figure 7 (Appendix D) visualizes ..."

   不合格的"引用"：
   ❌ 只在 paper 末尾甩一句 "See appendix for more details"（没指向具体 section）
   ❌ 在 Limitations 或 Conclusion 才提一次（应该在内容相关的章节就引出）
   ❌ 引用了但没说明为什么读者要去看（"see Appendix B" 没下文）

3. 修复策略（按优先级）
   - 优选：在最相关的正文章节加一句自然的引用。例如训练细节 → 在 Experimental Setup 末尾："For full training details including learning rate schedule and gradient clipping, see Appendix A."
   - 次选：如果实在找不到合适位置插入引用，且该附录内容对核心 claim 不必要，直接删除附录内容（不要保留无引用的"装饰性附录"）
   - 不要做：在某个章节最后硬塞一行 "See Appendix X." 当作补丁——这不算合理引用

4. 引用质量原则
   每条引用应该让 reviewer 立刻明白：
   - 为什么他要去翻那里（解决什么疑问 / 验证什么 claim）
   - 能在那里找到什么（具体是表、图、proof 还是定性例子）

---

完成后请汇报：

(a) 附录有多少个 section / subsection？分别是什么内容？
(b) 哪些原本没有正文引用？现在加在了哪个章节哪一句话？
(c) 是否有附录内容被你判定为"无必要"而删除？删掉了哪些？

2	\section{Confirmatory and Exploratory Analyses}\label{app:benchmark_details}
3	
4	The task taxonomy used in \S\ref{ssec:task_taxonomy} was defined prior to running any convergence analyses. Each benchmark was assigned to exactly one of four categories based on the primary cognitive demand of the task:
5	
6	\begin{itemize}
7	    \item \textbf{Retrieval}: tasks requiring prediction from long-range context without multi-step reasoning (LAMBADA).
8	    \item \textbf{Comprehension}: reading comprehension with explicit passage support (BoolQ).
9	    \item \textbf{Reasoning}: tasks requiring commonsense inference or multi-step reasoning (HellaSwag, ARC-Challenge, WinoGrande, PIQA).
10	    \item \textbf{Mixed}: tasks combining retrieval, comprehension, and reasoning demands (ARC-Easy, MMLU, SciQ, XStoryCloze).
11	\end{itemize}
12	
13	\paragraph{Confirmatory benchmark set.} Six benchmarks were designated as confirmatory for Bonferroni correction: LAMBADA, HellaSwag, ARC-Challenge, WinoGrande, ARC-Easy, and MMLU. These were selected as representative of retrieval, reasoning, and mixed categories before any convergence analysis was performed. The taxonomy and benchmark selection were fixed before analysis as an informal pre-analysis plan; we describe this as a confirmatory/exploratory split rather than a formal pre-registration, since no external timestamp exists. The main text applies the more conservative all-10-benchmark correction (Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .017 \times 10 = .170$). \textbf{Sensitivity:} correcting across only the six confirmatory benchmarks yields adjusted $p = .017 \times 6 = .100$, still above conventional significance.
14

101	At layer 2, GDN and RetNet produce nearly identical attention distributions (cosine 0.995), while HGRN2 diverges from both (cosine $< 0.25$). By layer 20, all three architectures converge to similar attention patterns (cosine $> 0.94$). The layer-wise trajectory suggests that early-layer attention patterns are shaped by the preceding recurrent layers (which differ across architectures), while deeper layers converge regardless of the recurrent substrate.
102	
103	\subsection{Helped vs.\ Hurt Attention Entropy}
104	
105	To test whether the attention mechanism itself differs between helped and hurt examples, we compute the Shannon entropy of the attention distribution for each example at each attention layer and compare the mean entropy of helped examples against hurt examples within each architecture.
106	
107	\begin{table}[ht]
108	\centering
109	\caption{Mean attention entropy difference (helped $-$ hurt) averaged across attention layers (1.3B h3-1 models on LAMBADA). Values near zero indicate that the attention mechanism does not discriminate between helped and hurt examples.}
110	\label{tab:attn_entropy}
111	\begin{tabular}{lr}
112	\toprule
113	Architecture & $\Delta H$ (helped $-$ hurt) \\
114	\midrule
115	GDN & $-0.0009$ \\
116	RetNet & $+0.0179$ \\
117	HGRN2 & $+0.0020$ \\
118	\bottomrule
119	\end{tabular}
120	\end{table}
121	
122	GDN and HGRN2 show negligible entropy differences ($|\Delta H| \leq 0.002$). RetNet's $\Delta H = +0.018$ is small in absolute terms but slightly larger; helped examples receive marginally more diffuse attention in RetNet. Across all three architectures, the differences are insufficient to explain the per-example divergence, consistent with per-example effects not being explained by attention weight patterns and likely originating in the recurrent layers.

215	\section{Normalized Jaccard Effect Size}\label{app:normalized_es}
216	
217	To assess whether low observed Jaccard reflects a floor effect imposed by unequal set sizes, we compute the theoretical upper bound $J_{\max} = \min(|A|,|B|) / \max(|A|,|B|)$ and the normalized effect size $(J_{\text{obs}} - J_{\text{null}}) / (J_{\max} - J_{\text{null}})$, which measures observed overlap as a fraction of the achievable range above chance. 299	
300	To assess whether the observed pairwise Jaccard similarities exceed what would be expected under independence, we perform a permutation test. For each architecture pair at 1.3B h3-1, we hold the helped (or hurt) set sizes fixed and randomly assign example indices to each set (10{,}000 permutations, seed 42). Table~\ref{tab:jaccard_perm} reports the observed $J$, the null baseline $J_{\text{null}}$ (mean over permutations), and the $z$-score.

The overlap between architectures' per-example effect sets is therefore structured rather than a product of random set overlap given the set sizes.

399	\section{Cross-Benchmark Jaccard Trajectories}\label{app:cross_bench_jaccard}
400	
401	To test whether the Jaccard stability pattern is specific to LAMBADA or a general property, we compute mean pairwise Jaccard trajectories across all four hybrid attention ratios on all four main benchmarks. Table~\ref{tab:cross_bench_jaccard} reports the results. On every benchmark, Jaccard shows no statistically significant trend with attention ratio (all $\rho \leq 0.80$, $p \geq 0.333$), with values uniformly between 0.08 and 0.11. The stability observed at the first attention level on LAMBADA (\S\ref{ssec:decoupling}) thus appears to hold across benchmarks: once any attention is present, each architecture's per-example response remains largely consistent regardless of attention ratio.

516	\subsection{Prediction Framework}\label{app:prediction_framework}
517	
518	We formalize the confidence margin analysis as a binary prediction task: given a pure-model example and its target-token $|\log p|$, predict whether attention will change the example's correctness. Using the within-category summary statistics (Tables~\ref{tab:cm_c1},~\ref{tab:cm_c2}), we derive analytic AUC from Cohen's $d$ via $\text{AUC} = \Phi(|d|/\sqrt{2})$ and compute Gaussian-intersection thresholds (the $|\log p|$ value at which the two group densities intersect under equal priors).
519	
520	Table~\ref{tab:prediction_auc} reports within-architecture prediction performance. All AUCs exceed 0.69, indicating moderate within-architecture predictive discrimination for attention effects. Disruption prediction is consistently stronger than rescue (mean AUC 0.73 vs.\ 0.70), reflecting the larger effect sizes in Control~1.

597	
598	\section{WinoGrande Supplementary Analysis}
599	\label{app:winogrande}
600	
601	WinoGrande ($N = 1{,}267$; reasoning, two-choice coreference resolution) shows high ambiguity ($\alpha \approx 0.41$ at h3-1) but mixed Jaccard significance: 10 of 24 architecture $\times$ attention-ratio pair combinations yield $p < .01$. Mean pairwise Jaccard ($\bar{J}_h = 0.110$, $\bar{J}_u = 0.103$) is comparable to the four main benchmarks, but the elevated null baseline from near-chance accuracy (${\sim}60\%$) reduces statistical power to detect above-chance overlap.
602	
603	\paragraph{Near-chance accuracy.} WinoGrande accuracy across hybrid configurations ranges from 59\% to 62\%, close to the two-choice chance level of 50\%. At near-chance accuracy, a large fraction of examples are borderline---small perturbations (such as adding attention) can flip predictions in either direction, inflating the helped and hurt sets and consequently $\alpha$. This structural property, shared with BoolQ (${\sim}59\%$ accuracy, $\alpha \approx 0.45$), may partially explain the elevated $\alpha$ rather than task-intrinsic multiplicity. PIQA (73\% accuracy, $\alpha = 0.14$) and HellaSwag (60\% normalized accuracy, $\alpha = 0.15$) demonstrate that near-chance accuracy is not the sole driver, but we cannot fully exclude it as a contributing factor for WinoGrande and BoolQ.

1	% Appendix sections for the paper.
2	
3	\section{Confirmatory and Exploratory Analyses}\label{app:benchmark_details}
4	
5	The task taxonomy used in \S\ref{ssec:task_taxonomy} was defined prior to running any convergence analyses. Each benchmark was assigned to exactly one of four categories based on the primary cognitive demand of the task:
6	
7	\begin{itemize}
8	    \item \textbf{Retrieval}: tasks requiring prediction from long-range context without multi-step reasoning (LAMBADA).
9	    \item \textbf{Comprehension}: reading comprehension with explicit passage support (BoolQ).
10	    \item \textbf{Reasoning}: tasks requiring commonsense inference or multi-step reasoning (HellaSwag, ARC-Challenge, WinoGrande, PIQA).
11	    \item \textbf{Mixed}: tasks combining retrieval, comprehension, and reasoning demands (ARC-Easy, MMLU, SciQ, XStoryCloze).
12	\end{itemize}
13	
14	\paragraph{Confirmatory benchmark set.} Six benchmarks were designated as confirmatory for Bonferroni correction: LAMBADA, HellaSwag, ARC-Challenge, WinoGrande, ARC-Easy, and MMLU. These were selected as representative of retrieval, reasoning, and mixed categories before any convergence analysis was performed. The taxonomy and benchmark selection were fixed before analysis as an informal pre-analysis plan; we describe this as a confirmatory/exploratory split rather than a formal pre-registration, since no external timestamp exists. The main text applies the more conservative all-10-benchmark correction (Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .017 \times 10 = .170$). \textbf{Sensitivity:} correcting across only the six confirmatory benchmarks yields adjusted $p = .017 \times 6 = .100$, still above conventional significance.
15	
16	\paragraph{One-sided test.} If one adopts the prior that attention should reduce cross-architecture variance, the one-sided permutation $p$-value for LAMBADA is $1/120 \approx 0.0083$. Under 10-benchmark Bonferroni correction, this yields an adjusted $p = 0.083$, above the conventional thre

31	\title{Aggregate Equivalence $\neq$ Behavioral Equivalence: Per-Example Multiplicity in Hybrid Linear Recurrent Models}
32	
33	\author{Anonymous}
34	
35	\begin{document}
36	\maketitle
37	
38	\begin{abstract}
39	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\approx 0.10$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\% attention and remains flat across higher ratios, suggesting that minimal attention may establish which examples each arch

1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures---including GatedDeltaNet (GDN), RetNet, HGRN2, Gated Linear Attention (GLA), and DeltaNet---achieve aggregate benchmark scores within 1--2 points of each other at matched parameter budgets, yet they can differ on up to 90\% of the individual examples they affect. This per-example divergence is invisible in aggregate metrics: architectures that score similarly reach that score by gaining and losing on different inputs, and the choice of backbone carries hidden consequences for which specific examples a deployed system handles correctly.
4	
5	\begin{figure*}[t]
6	\centering
7	\begin{minipage}[t]{0.56\textwidth}
8	\centering

10	\end{minipage}%
11	\hfill

13	\centering
14	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig4_jaccard_saturation.pdf}
15	\end{minipage}
16	\caption{Per-example overlap appears stable from minimal attention across benchmarks. (a,\,b) LAMBADA: cross-architecture $\sigma$ shows a non-significant decreasing trend at 1.3B (solid; Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$) but partially rebounds at 340M (dashed), while pairwise Jaccard of helped/hurt sets appears stable from 4\% attention onward. (c) This pattern holds across all four main benchmarks: mean pairwise $\bar{J}$ remains flat across attention ratios (all $\rho \leq 0.80$, $p \geq .333$). Shaded bands are illustrative ($n = 3$).}
17	\label{fig:decoupling}
18	\end{figure*}
19	
20	This tension arises in the context of a rapid architectural shift toward hybrid recurrent-attention models, which interleave attention layers within linear recurrent backbones. Jamba \citep{lieber2024jamba}, Griffin \citep{de2024griffin}, RWKV-6 \citep{peng2024eagle}, and RecurrentGemma \citep{botev2024recurrentgemma} each combine recurrence with attention using different mixing strategies. 2	
3	\paragraph{Hybrid Recurrent Architectures.}
4	The design space for combining attention with recurrence spans layer-level interleaving \citep[Jamba;][]{lieber2024jamba}, local attention pairing \citep[Griffin, RecurrentGemma;][]{de2024griffin,botev2024recurrentgemma}, matrix-valued recurrence \citep[RWKV-6;][]{peng2024eagle}, within-head mixing \citep[Hymba;][]{dong2024hymba}, and unbounded-context hybrid SSMs \citep[Samba;][]{ren2024samba}. H3 \citep{fu2023hungry} and Hyena \citep{poli2023hyena} establish early design points in the hybrid space with gated convolutions and subquadratic operators. Mamba-2 \citep{dao2024mamba2} establishes a duality between structured state spaces and attention; Mamba-3 \citep{lahoti2026mamba3} extends the design further, while \citet{ali2025hidden} reveal implicit attention within Mamba. Comparative evaluations confirm that hybrids outperform pure Transformers and SSMs at scale \citep{waleffe2024empirical}, and that even one attention layer enables content-based routing \citep{basu2026routing}. Recent theoretical work establishes a provable expressiveness hierarchy between hybrid and pure linear attention \citep{ye2026provable}, providing formal grounding for the architectural differences we characterize at the per-example level. \citet{wang2025hybrid} provide the controlled model zoo we use, holding training conditions constant while varying architecture and attention ratio.
5	
6	\paragraph{Linear Recurrent Models.}
7	Our three recurrent backbones represent distinct gating strategies within the linear recurrence family: RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet} uses multi-scale exponential decay, HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} applies gated state expansion, and GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet} combines gated updates with delta rules. These descend from S4 \citep{gu2022s4} and Mamba \citep{gu2024mamba}, which introduced structured state spaces and input-dependent selection, respectively, and connect to linear a
…[truncated 1516 chars]

The GDN 1.3B h3-1 configuration uses 16 attention heads vs.\ 32 for RetNet and HGRN2 (\S\ref{ssec:robustness}). We adopt the h$k$-1 notation: h3-1 = 25\% attention, h6-1 = 14.3\%, h12-1 = 7.7\%, h24-1 = 4\%. Within each scale, all models share identical training data, tokenizer, and hyperparameters, isolating architecture and attention configuration. The zoo also includes a Transformer baseline at both scales.

1	\vspace{-2pt}
2	\section{Conclusion}
3	
4	We have shown that hybrid linear recurrent architectures which appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks diverge substantially at the per-example level. This multiplicity varies by task type ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\% attention (pairwise Jaccard $\leq 0.10$); two interpretations---architecture-specific fingerprints or rapid convergence toward Transformer-like behavior---remain open (Appendix~\ref{app:transformer_entrainment}). Confidence margins partially predict which examples are affected (AUC 0.70--0.74), with rescue thresholds architecture-invariant but disruption thresholds varying 47\%.
5	
6	At the 340M--1.3B scales studied here, per-example audit before architecture substitution is advisable when multiplicity is substantial---aggregate equivalence does not guarantee behavioral equivalence. Low cross-architecture Jaccard creates complementarity ($+7$--$14$\,pp oracle gain), though preliminary routing experiments show exploitation remains task-dependent ($+5.3$\,pp on LAMBADA; $-0.6$\,pp on PIQA).
7

78	
79	\subsection{Per-Example Overlap Appears Stable from Minimal Attention}\label{ssec:decoupling}
80	
81	Does adding more attention change \emph{which} examples each architecture affects? Pairwise Jaccard appears to stabilize at the first attention level (4\%; $\bar{J} \approx 0.095$) and remains flat across all higher ratios (Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling}b), suggesting that minimal attention may suffice to establish the per-example divergence pattern, though the small number of ratios ($n = 5$) limits statistical confirmation. As background, LAMBADA's aggregate cross-architecture $\sigma$ shows a non-significant decreasing trend ($\rho = -1.0$, Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$; excluding GDN: $\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$; MMLU reverses to $\rho = +0.6$ when excluding GDN; observed at 1.3B, absent at 340M; Table~\ref{tab:convergence}, Figure~\ref{fig:task_specificity}). No other benchmark shows a comparable aggregate pattern ($n = 5$; negligible power for $|\rho| < 1.0$).

112	Cross-scale analysis reveals a clear dissociation: per-example Jaccard is consistent across both scales ($\bar{J} = 0.08$--$0.10$ at 340M and 1.3B), whereas the aggregate variance trend is observed only at 1.3B on LAMBADA ($\rho = -0.50$, $p = .45$ at 340M). Ambiguity $\alpha$ is systematically higher at 340M ($\Delta \leq 8$\,pp), suggesting that the \emph{pattern} of per-example multiplicity is stable across scales while its magnitude tracks model capacity (Appendix~\ref{app:scale_per_example}--\ref{app:cross_scale}).
113	\vspace{-2pt}

[Audit 回复]:
[PAPER AUDIT] entrainment_gated_ft 论文质量审计报告

━━━ 确定性检查 ━━━
编译: ✅
页数: 21
源码: 2 critical, 89 warning

━━━ 内容结构 ━━━
- [info] [叙事一致性] Abstract→Intro→Method→Experiments→Conclusion 的核心故事高度一致：问题（聚合等价掩盖逐样本差异）、方法（受控模型动物园 + 逐样本分解）、发现（结构化预测多重性依赖任务类型而非注意力比例）、意义（互补性 + 路由可能性）。未检测到叙事漂移。
- [info] [叙事一致性] Intro 三条贡献分别对应 §4.1（per-example divergence + multiplicity variation）、§4.2（decoupling）、§4.1+App F（confidence margins），Section/Table 映射完整。
- [warning] [叙事一致性] Conclusion 直接重复了 Results 的具体数字（α=0.14–0.30, J≤0.10, AUC 0.70–0.74, +5.3pp, -0.6pp）而非引用表格编号。按 paper-quality-lessons §3.1，Discussion/Conclusion 应引用 Table 编号而非重复数字。且 Conclusion 缺少更高层解读（对 hybrid 架构选择实践的影响、对 benchmark 设计的启示、与更广泛的模型选择问题的联系）。
- [warning] [叙事一致性] 标题中的 "Behavioral Equivalence" 是一个吸引人的概念，但该术语在正文中仅在 Conclusion 最后一句出现一次（"aggregate equivalence does not guarantee behavioral equivalence"）。建议在 Introduction 中也使用此术语建立概念框架，强化标题与正文的呼应。
- [info] [章节结构] Abstract 四问完整回答：问题（聚合等价掩盖差异）、方法（受控模型动物园 + 逐样本分解）、结果（Jaccard≈0.10, α=0.14–0.30, 4%注意力稳定化）、意义（互补性 + 路由依赖任务）。约153词，结构紧凑。
- [info] [章节结构] Introduction 结构良好：¶1 核心发现开场 → ¶2 背景（hybrid 架构快速发展）→ ¶3 Gap（multiplicity 未研究架构变异）→ ¶4 本文工作 → ¶5 核心发现 → 贡献列表。非传统五段但逻辑清晰。
- [info] [章节结构] Related Work 按主题分类（Hybrid Architectures / Linear Recurrent Models / Underspecification and Predictive Multiplicity），非堆砌式。Positioning 清晰。
- [info] [贡献对齐] 三条贡献均有对应 section/table 支撑。无孤儿章节。
- [info] [贡献对齐] Abstract 的范围词（"three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales"）与实验匹配。"five architectures" 用于五架构验证也准确（三主 + GLA + DeltaNet at 340M）。
- [info] [Claim强度] 未使用 "state-of-the-art"、"significantly outperforms" 等过强表述。所有 Claim 均有适当 hedging（"appears to stabilize"、"partially predict"、"non-significant decreasing trend"）。
- [info] [Claim强度] "all p < .01" 有统计检验支撑（排列检验 z > 5.9）。"roughly twice the independence baseline" 对应 J≈0.10 vs J_null≈0.05，准确。
- [warning] [Claim强度] introduction.tex:3 "can differ on up to 90% of the individual examples they affect" — 这个数字来自 1-J ≈ 0.90（即 ~90% non-overlap），但 "differ on up to 90%" 的表述可能被误读为"90%的样本上给出不同答案"。实际上 J≈0.10 衡量的是 helped/hurt 集合的重叠度，不是总体样本的不一致率。建议改为更精确的表述，如 "the helped/hurt example sets overlap by only ~10%"。
- [warning] [实验严谨性] 所有配置来自单次训练运行（无 error bars / std），Limitations 已声明。Table 2（convergence）和 Table 3（multiplicity）均无 error bars。Bootstrap CI 仅在 appendix 提供且标注为 "heuristic"（n=3）。单 seed 实验的数据支撑强度按 paper-quality-lessons §2.1 仅允许 "preliminary observation" / "initial evidence" 级别表述，但正文多处使用了 "structured" 等较强限定词。
- [info] [实验严谨性] Baseline 引用近年论文：Wang et al. (2025), Yang et al. (2026), Marx et al. (2020), D'Amour et al. (2022) 等。时效性充足。
- [info] [实验严谨性] Ablation 覆盖：GDN 排除敏感性、五架构验证、连续 Jaccard、跨尺度分析、seed 校准（Pythia）。与方法组件数（per-example decomposition, convergence metrics, confidence margins）大致匹配。
- [warning] [可复现性] 论文正文和 appendix 均未披露计算资源信息（GPU 型号、推理时间、总 GPU-hours）。Author response 提到 "single NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000, ~5 GPU-hours inference-only"，但这不在 PDF 中。EMNLP checklist 要求此信息。建议在 Appendix 或 Method 中添加一段计算资源说明。
- [info] [可复现性] lm-evaluation-harness 版本（v0.4.x）已声明；bootstrap seed（42）已声明；排列检验参数（10,000 次）已声明。模型来自公开模型动物园。代码承诺发布。
- [info] [符号一致性] 核心符号（α, J, σ, ρ, h_k-1）全文定义一致、使用规范。新符号首次出现时均有定义（如 §3.3 定义 σ, §3.4 定义 helped/hurt/unchanged, §3.5 定义检验方法）。
- [warning] [术语一致性] "convergence" 一词在论文中有双重含义：(1) 聚合层面的 σ 减小（aggregate convergence），(2) 向 Transformer 行为趋同（convergence toward Transformer-like behavior, App G）。§4.2 试图通过 "decoupling" 概念区分两者，但读者首次遇到时可能困惑。建议在 §3.3 首次使用 "convergence" 时明确其仅指聚合 σ 减小，并为第二种含义使用不同术语（如 "entrainment"，正好是 App G 的标题）。
- [info] [过渡] Method→Experiments 过渡良好（§4 开头 "Using the model zoo of [citation] (§3.1)"回顾设置）。
- [warning] [过渡] Experiments §4.1 → §4.2 的过渡缺失。§4.1 结束于 multiplicity variation 讨论，§4.2 直接以问句 "Does adding more attention change which examples each architecture affects?" 开头。虽然问句式开头可接受，但两节间缺少衔接句说明从"多少"multiplicity 转向"是否随注意力变化"的逻辑推进。
- [info] [过渡] 论文无独立 Discussion 节。Interpretation（fingerprint vs convergence）嵌入 §4.2。对紧凑型实证论文可接受。
- [info] [Limitations] 4 个不同的 limitation 段落：Single training runs / Format confound / Coverage / Pre-training only。约 159 词。实质性且非回避式（每段指向具体未解决问题而非笼统的 "future work"）。

1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures---including GatedDeltaNet (GDN), RetNet, HGRN2, Gated Linear Attention (GLA), and DeltaNet---achieve aggregate benchmark scores within 1--2 points of each other at matched parameter budgets, yet they can differ on up to 90\% of the individual examples they affect. This per-example divergence is invisible in aggregate metrics: architectures that score similarly reach that score by gaining and losing on different inputs, and the choice of backbone carries hidden consequences for which specific examples a deployed system handles correctly.

16	\caption{Per-example overlap appears stable from minimal attention across benchmarks. (a,\,b) LAMBADA: cross-architecture $\sigma$ shows a non-significant decreasing trend at 1.3B (solid; Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$) but partially rebounds at 340M (dashed), while pairwise Jaccard of helped/hurt sets appears stable from 4\% attention onward. (c) This pattern holds across all four main benchmarks: mean pairwise $\bar{J}$ remains flat across attention ratios (all $\rho \leq 0.80$, $p \geq .333$). Shaded bands are illustrative ($n = 3$).}
17	\label{fig:decoupling}
18	\end{figure*}

20	This tension arises in the context of a rapid architectural shift toward hybrid recurrent-attention models, which interleave attention layers within linear recurrent backbones. Jamba \citep{lieber2024jamba}, Griffin \citep{de2024griffin}, RWKV-6 \citep{peng2024eagle}, and RecurrentGemma \citep{botev2024recurrentgemma} each combine recurrence with attention using different mixing strategies. _source line 483 · 2026-05-25T09:14:35.708355+00:00_

53	
54	\paragraph{Confidence margin predictor.} Pure-model confidence on the target token predicts which examples will be affected (AUC 0.70--0.74, a non-parametric measure equivalent to Mann-Whitney $U$; Appendix~\ref{app:confidence_margin}): rescue thresholds are architecture-invariant (1\% spread) but disruption thresholds vary 47\%, explaining cross-architecture Jaccard divergence (Appendix~\ref{app:prediction_framework}). Calibration differences account for less than half the threshold spread (Appendix~\ref{app:calibration}). The rescue pattern generalizes across three benchmarks ($d = 0.13$--$0.60$; Appendix~\ref{app:cm_cross}); disruption is weaker on reasoning tasks (Appendix~\ref{app:boolq}). This structured disagreement constitutes predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive} under architectural variation.
55	
56	\paragraph{Confidence-based routing.} As an illustrative case study, the rescue-threshold asymmetry suggests a routing strategy: select the architecture whose pure-model confidence best predicts successful rescue. On LAMBADA, this outperforms majority vote by $+5.3$\,pp (SD $= 0.4$\,pp, 10 cross-validated splits), extracting 36\% of the oracle gap (oracle = 65.5\%; Appendix~\ref{app:complementarity}). On ARC-Easy, gains are modest ($+1.0$\,pp, SD $= 0.5$\,pp); on HellaSwag, routing provides no benefit ($-0.02$\,pp, $p = .89$); on PIQA, it is significantly negative ($-0.6$\,pp, $p = .006$). Routing works when multiplicity is high ($\alpha = 0.30$, LAMBADA) and a clear rescue-threshold signal exists, but is counterproductive when multiplicity is low ($\alpha = 0.14$, PIQA). A natural comparison---average-logits ensemble---is left to future work as it requires simultaneous inference across all architectures.
57

_source line 487 · 2026-05-25T09:14:46.904863+00:00_

108	\end{figure}
109	
110	Two interpretations are consistent with the observed stability. \textbf{(a) Fingerprint view:} each recurrent backbone imposes a characteristic inductive bias that determines which examples attention can rescue, locking in architecture-specific sensitivity patterns. \textbf{(b) Convergence view:} even minimal attention causes all architectures to rapidly adopt Transformer-like behavior, with residual per-example divergence arising from incomplete convergence. Appendix~\ref{app:transformer_entrainment} shows that h3-1 correct sets indeed shift toward the Transformer baseline, providing partial support for interpretation (b). Disentangling these accounts requires ablation of the attention mechanism itself---for example, replacing learned attention weights with random projections---an important direction for future work. Attention weight analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:attention_patterns}) shows GDN and RetNet produce nearly identical attention distributions (cosine 0.995) yet differ in per-example effects, suggesting these originate in the recurrent layers. The four-benchmark variation (Table~\ref{tab:multiplicity}) is consistent with independence: LAMBADA ($\alpha = 0.30$) is the only benchmark showing a variance-reduction trend despite having the highest multiplicity, while PIQA ($\alpha = 0.14$) and HellaSwag ($\alpha = 0.15$) show no variance reduction with the lowest multiplicity.
111	
112	Cross-scale analysis reveals a clear dissociation: per-example Jaccard is consistent across both scales ($\bar{J} = 0.08$--$0.10$ at 340M and 1.3B), whereas the aggregate variance trend is observed only at 1.3B on LAMBADA ($\rho = -0.50$, $p = .45$ at 340M). Ambiguity $\alpha$ is systematically higher at 340M ($\Delta \leq 8$\,pp), suggesting that the \emph{pattern} of per-example multiplicity is stable across scales while its magnitude tracks model capacity (Appendices~\ref{app:scale_convergence}, \ref{app:scale_per_example}--\ref{app:cross_scale}).

1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Hybrid Recurrent Architectures.}
4	The design space for combining attention with recurrence spans layer-level interleaving \citep[Jamba;][]{lieber2024jamba}, local attention pairing \citep[Griffin, RecurrentGemma;][]{de2024griffin,botev2024recurrentgemma}, matrix-valued recurrence \citep[RWKV-6;][]{peng2024eagle}, within-head mixing \citep[Hymba;][]{dong2024hymba}, and unbounded-context hybrid SSMs \citep[Samba;][]{ren2024samba}. H3 \citep{fu2023hungry} and Hyena \citep{poli2023hyena} establish early design points in the hybrid space with gated convolutions and subquadratic operators. Mamba-2 \citep{dao2024mamba2} establishes a duality between structured state spaces and attention; Mamba-3 \citep{lahoti2026mamba3} extends the design further, while \citet{ali2025hidden} reveal implicit attention within Mamba. Comparative evaluations confirm that hybrids outperform pure Transformers and SSMs at scale \citep{waleffe2024empirical}, and that even one attention layer enables content-based routing \citep{basu2026routing}. Recent theoretical work establishes a provable expressiveness hierarchy between hybrid and pure linear attention \citep{ye2026provable}, providing formal grounding for the architectural differences we characterize at the per-example level. \citet{wang2025hybrid} provide the controlled model zoo we use, holding training conditions constant while varying architecture and attention ratio.
5	
6	\paragraph{Linear Recurrent Models.}
7	Our three recurrent backbones represent distinct gating strategies within the linear recurrence family: RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet} uses multi-scale exponential decay, HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} applies gated state expansion, and GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet} combines gated updates with delta rules. These descend from S4 \citep{gu2022s4} and Mamba \citep{gu2024mamba}, which introduced structured state spaces and input-dependent selection, respectively, and connect to linear a

78	
79	\subsection{Per-Example Overlap Appears Stable from Minimal Attention}\label{ssec:decoupling}
80	
81	Does adding more attention change \emph{which} examples each architecture affects? Pairwise Jaccard appears to stabilize at the first attention level (4\%; $\bar{J} \approx 0.095$) and remains flat across all higher ratios (Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling}b), suggesting that minimal attention may suffice to establish the per-example divergence pattern, though the small number of ratios ($n = 5$) limits statistical confirmation. As background, LAMBADA's aggregate cross-architecture $\sigma$ shows a non-significant decreasing trend ($\rho = -1.0$, Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$; excluding GDN: $\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$; MMLU reverses to $\rho = +0.6$ when excluding GDN; observed at 1.3B, absent at 340M; Table~\ref{tab:convergence}, Figure~\ref{fig:task_specificity}). No other benchmark shows a comparable aggregate pattern ($n = 5$; negligible power for $|\rho| < 1.0$; Appendix~\ref{app:convergence_detail}).

777	% Figure moved to main text (\S\ref{ssec:robustness})
778	
779	All pairwise Jaccard values remain $\leq 0.14$, consistent with the three-architecture results at 1.3B (Table~\ref{tab:per_example}). The seven new pairs involving GLA and DeltaNet show no systematic difference from the original three pairs (Mann-Whitney $p > .3$ for both helped and hurt), indicating that the low-overlap pattern is not specific to the original architecture triple. A sixth architecture (HGRN; \citealp{qin2023hierarchically}) is also available in the zoo but excluded from the main analysis: its substantially lower baseline accuracy on LAMBADA (0.263 vs.\ 0.32--0.38 for the other five) mechanically inflates helped-set sizes and Jaccard values. All 15 six-architecture permutation tests nonetheless remain significant ($z \geq 2.9$, $p \leq .006$). The same pattern holds on HellaSwag ($N = 10{,}042$) and PIQA ($N = 1{,}838$): all 20 additional pairwise tests yield $p < .01$ with mean $J_{\text{helped}} \leq 0.10$. Full four-benchmark and six-architecture data are included in our code release.

1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures (GatedDeltaNet, RetNet, HGRN2, GLA, DeltaNet) achieve aggregate benchmark scores within 1--2 points of each other at matched parameter budgets, yet their helped and hurt example sets overlap by only ${\sim}10$\% (pairwise Jaccard $\approx 0.10$). This gap between aggregate equivalence and per-example behavioral equivalence is invisible in standard metrics: architectures that score similarly reach that score by gaining and losing on different inputs, and the choice of backbone carries hidden consequences for which specific examples a deployed system handles correctly.
4	
5	\begin{figure*}[t]
6	\centering
7	\begin{minipage}[t]{0.56\textwidth}
8	\centering
9	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig1_decoupling_hero.pdf}
10	\end{minipage}%
11	\hfill
12	\begin{minipage}[t]{0.42\textwidth}
13	\centering
14	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig4_jaccard_saturation.pdf}
15	\end{minipage}
16	\caption{Per-example overlap appears stable from minimal attention across benchmarks. (a,\,b) LAMBADA: cross-architecture $\sigma$ shows a non-significant decreasing trend at 1.3B (solid; Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$) but partially rebounds at 340M (dashed), while pairwise Jaccard of helped/hurt sets appears stable from 4\% attention onward. (c) This pattern holds across all four main benchmarks: mean pairwise $\bar{J}$ remains flat across attention ratios (all $\rho \leq 0.80$, $p \geq .333$). Shaded bands are illustrative ($n = 3$).}
17	\label{fig:decoupling}
18	\end{figure*}
19	
20	This tension arises in the context of a rapid architectural shift toward hybrid recurrent-attention models, which interleave attention layers within linear recurrent backbones. Jamba \citep{lieber2024jamba}, Griffin \citep{de2024griffin}, RWKV-6 \citep{peng2024eagle}, and RecurrentGemma \citep{botev2024recurrentgemma} each combine recurrence with attention using different mixing strategies. 1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures \citep[GatedDeltaNet,][]{yang2024gateddeltanet}, \citep[RetNet,][]{sun2023retnet}, \citep[HGRN2,][]{qin2024hgrn2}, \citep[GLA,][]{yang2024gla}, \citep[DeltaNet;][]{yang2024deltanet}) achieve aggregate benchmark scores within 1--2 points of each other at matched parameter budgets, yet their helped and hurt example sets overlap by only ${\sim}10$\% (pairwise Jaccard $\approx 0.10$). This gap between aggregate equivalence and per-example behavioral equivalence is invisible in standard metrics: architectures that score similarly reach that score by gaining and losing on different inputs, and the choice of backbone carries hidden consequences for which specific examples a deployed system handles correctly.
4

_source line 549 · 2026-05-25T09:36:40.808269+00:00_

22	Prior work on predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive} and underspecification \citep{damour2022underspecification,mccoy2020berts} documents this risk for seed variation within a single architecture, but whether it extends to architectural variation in hybrid recurrent models remains open. If architectures that score similarly in aggregate behave differently on individual inputs, the backbone choice carries hidden consequences for deployment reliability and ensemble construction.
23	
24	We present the first controlled within-family per-example analysis of how attention affects cross-architecture agreement in hybrid recurrent models. Using the model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid}, we compare three architectures (GatedDeltaNet, RetNet, HGRN2) at five attention ratios (0--25\%) and two scales (340M, 1.3B). We evaluate all 30 configurations on 10 benchmarks spanning retrieval, comprehension, reasoning, and mixed categories. On four benchmarks (LAMBADA, $N = 5{,}153$; HellaSwag, $N = 10{,}042$; PIQA, $N = 1{,}838$; ARC-Easy, $N = 2{,}376$) we conduct per-example decompositions, classifying each example as helped, hurt, or unchanged when attention is added, and measure cross-architecture overlap via Jaccard similarity. Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling} previews our central finding: architectures that appear equivalent by aggregate benchmarks diverge systematically at the per-example level, and this divergence is established by minimal attention.
25	
26	Our central finding is that architectural variation produces structured predictive multiplicity whose magnitude depends on task type, not attention ratio. The ambiguity $\alpha$ \citep{marx2020predictive} ranges from 0.14 (PIQA) to 0.30 (LAMBADA), varies $2\times$ across task types but only 0.03 within each benchmark across attention ratios; pairwise Jaccard remains uniformly low ($\leq 0.10$, all $p < .01$ across all ten five-architecture pairs). A supporting observation is that per-example overlap appears to stabiliz

40	
41	\subsection{Per-Example Divergence}\label{ssec:per_example}
42	
43	Do architectures converge by improving on the same examples? We compare each architecture's pure-recurrent baseline against its h3-1 hybrid on 5{,}153 LAMBADA examples, classifying each as helped, hurt, or unchanged. Although the decomposition is binary, it captures genuine separation in the continuous confidence space (Cohen's $d = 0.73$--$0.92$ between helped and hurt on LAMBADA; cross-benchmark details in Appendix~\ref{app:cm_cross}). Binarization discards confidence magnitude, so we supplement with continuous confidence margin analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:confidence_margin}) and verify that low Jaccard persists under continuous thresholds (Appendix~\ref{app:continuous_jaccard}).
44	
45	Each architecture displays a distinct profile of per-example changes (Figure~\ref{fig:per_example_bars}). GDN loses a net 153 examples (462 helped, 615 hurt), RetNet loses 69 (426/495), and HGRN2 gains 153 (548/395; full counts in Appendix~\ref{app:per_example_counts}). HGRN2 is the only architecture for which adding attention yields a net per-example benefit. Pairwise Jaccard similarity is low: mean $\bar{J} \leq 0.10$, indicating ${\sim}90$\% non-overlap. Overlap significantly exceeds the independence baseline ($J_{\text{null}} \approx 0.05$, all $z > 5.9$, $p < .0001$; Appendix~\ref{app:jaccard_permutation}), plausibly reflecting shared sensitivity to example difficulty. Normalizing by $J_{\max}$, observed overlap reaches only 6--7\% of the achievable range above chance (Appendix~\ref{app:normalized_es}), confirming that low Jaccard cannot be attributed to set-size constraints.

96	
97	Each architecture's per-example response to attention is largely idiosyncratic. The observed $\bar{J}$ reaches only 6--7\% of the achievable range above chance ($J_{\max} \approx 0.78$--$0.92$), ruling out ceiling constraints (\S\ref{ssec:per_example}). Readers can verify $\rho$ directly from Table~\ref{tab:convergence}; with $n = 3$ architectures, all bootstrap CIs are heuristic indicators of direction rather than precise coverage.
98	
99	This pattern generalizes beyond LAMBADA (Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling}c): on all four main benchmarks, mean pairwise Jaccard shows no significant trend with attention ratio (all $\rho \leq 0.80$, $p \geq 0.333$), with values uniformly between 0.08 and 0.11 (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_bench_jaccard}).
100	
101	Heuristic bootstrap 95\% CIs ($n = 3$; \S\ref{ssec:statistical_methods}) show non-overlapping $\sigma$ between pure and h3-1 but completely overlapping Jaccard across all hybrid ratios (\S\ref{ssec:statistical_methods}). Figure~\ref{fig:jaccard_hierarchy} shows a three-tier Jaccard hierarchy: independence baseline (${\sim}0.05$), cross-architecture (${\sim}0.10$, $2\times$ baseline), and within-architecture (${\sim}0.33$, $3\times$ cross-architecture; Appendix~\ref{app:within_arch_stability}). Pythia 70M seed calibration yields a comparable within-seed level ($J = 0.27$--$0.39$; Appendix~\ref{app:seed_calibration}), consistent with architecture-associated divergence, though matched multi-seed experiments would be needed for definitive evidence.

Pythia 70M within-seed Jaccard (different scale) falls at a comparable level. Error bars show the helped--hurt range.}

1	\section{Method}\label{sec:method}
2	
3	We describe the model configurations, evaluation benchmarks, and analytical framework used to measure how attention affects cross-architecture agreement at both aggregate and per-example levels.
4

24	\subsection{Model Zoo}\label{ssec:model_zoo}
25	
26	We use the pretrained model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid} (Table~\ref{tab:model_zoo}), which provides controlled comparisons: GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet}, RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet}, and HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} at five attention ratios and two scales. The GDN 1.3B h3-1 configuration uses 16 attention heads vs.\ 32 for RetNet and HGRN2 (\S\ref{ssec:robustness}). We adopt the h$k$-1 notation: h3-1 = 25\% attention, h6-1 = 14.3\%, h12-1 = 7.7\%, h24-1 = 4\%. Within each scale, all models share identical training data, tokenizer, and hyperparameters, isolating architecture and attention configuration. The zoo also includes a Transformer baseline at both scales.
27	
28	\subsection{Task Taxonomy}\label{ssec:task_taxonomy}

_source line 583 · 2026-05-25T09:38:51.782252+00:00_

99	This pattern generalizes beyond LAMBADA (Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling}c): on all four main benchmarks, mean pairwise Jaccard shows no significant trend with attention ratio (all $\rho \leq 0.80$, $p \geq 0.333$), with values uniformly between 0.08 and 0.11 (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_bench_jaccard}).
100	
101	Heuristic bootstrap 95\% CIs ($n = 3$; \S\ref{ssec:statistical_methods}) show non-overlapping $\sigma$ between pure and h3-1 but completely overlapping Jaccard across all hybrid ratios (\S\ref{ssec:statistical_methods}). Figure~\ref{fig:jaccard_hierarchy} shows a three-tier Jaccard hierarchy: independence baseline (${\sim}0.05$), cross-architecture (${\sim}0.10$, $2\times$ baseline), and within-architecture (${\sim}0.33$, $3\times$ cross-architecture; Appendix~\ref{app:within_arch_stability}). Pythia 70M seed calibration yields a comparable within-seed level ($J = 0.27$--$0.39$; Appendix~\ref{app:seed_calibration}), consistent with architecture-associated divergence, though matched multi-seed experiments would be needed for definitive evidence.
102	
103	\begin{figure}[t]

### Tool result

_source line 587 · 2026-05-25T09:39:03.296210+00:00_

28	Our contributions are as follows:
29	\begin{itemize}
30	    \item Architectural variation in hybrid recurrent models, controlled within a single model zoo sharing training data and hyperparameters, produces structured predictive multiplicity whose magnitude varies by task type (or evaluation format) rather than attention ratio (\S\ref{ssec:per_example}). Five-architecture validation confirms the pattern (\S\ref{ssec:robustness}; Appendix~\ref{app:extended_arch}).
31	    \item Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at the first attention level (4\%) across all four benchmarks: minimal attention may establish which examples each architecture affects, with no observed homogenization at higher ratios ($n = 5$ ratios; \S\ref{ssec:decoupling}).
32	    \item Pure-model confidence margins partially predict which examples attention will affect (AUC 0.70--0.74), with rescue thresholds architecture-invariant but disruption thresholds varying 47\% across architectures, constituting a second dimension of multiplicity beyond per-example disagreement (\S\ref{ssec:per_example}; Appendix~\ref{app:prediction_framework}).
33	\end{itemize}
34	\noindent Code and data are available at \url{https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026-2/entrainment_gated_ft}.

_source line 607 · 2026-05-25T09:40:52.553216+00:00_

1	\vspace{-2pt}
2	\section{Conclusion}
3	
4	We have shown that hybrid linear recurrent architectures which appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks diverge substantially at the per-example level. This multiplicity varies by task type ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\% attention (pairwise Jaccard $\leq 0.10$); two interpretations (architecture-specific fingerprints or rapid convergence toward Transformer-like behavior) remain open (Appendix~\ref{app:transformer_entrainment}). Confidence margins partially predict which examples are affected (AUC 0.70--0.74), with rescue thresholds architecture-invariant but disruption thresholds varying 47\%.
5	
6	At the 340M--1.3B scales studied here, per-example audit before architecture substitution is advisable when multiplicity is substantial; aggregate equivalence does not guarantee behavioral equivalence. Low cross-architecture Jaccard creates complementarity ($+7$--$14$\,pp oracle gain), though preliminary routing experiments show exploitation remains task-dependent ($+5.3$\,pp on LAMBADA; $-0.6$\,pp on PIQA).
7

_source line 633 · 2026-05-25T09:45:04.709511+00:00_

45	Each architecture displays a distinct profile of per-example changes (Figure~\ref{fig:per_example_bars}). GDN loses a net 153 examples (462 helped, 615 hurt), RetNet loses 69 (426/495), and HGRN2 gains 153 (548/395; full counts in Appendix~\ref{app:per_example_counts}). HGRN2 is the only architecture for which adding attention yields a net per-example benefit. Pairwise Jaccard similarity is low: mean $\bar{J} \leq 0.10$, indicating ${\sim}90$\% non-overlap. Overlap significantly exceeds the independence baseline ($J_{\text{null}} \approx 0.05$, all $z > 5.9$, $p < .0001$; Appendix~\ref{app:jaccard_permutation}), plausibly reflecting shared sensitivity to example difficulty. Normalizing by $J_{\max}$, observed overlap reaches only 6--7\% of the achievable range above chance (Appendix~\ref{app:normalized_es}), confirming that low Jaccard cannot be attributed to set-size constraints.
46	
47	\begin{figure}[t]
48	\centering
49	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_alpha_routing.pdf}
50	\caption{(a) Per-example ambiguity $\alpha$ varies $2\times$ across task types. (b) Confidence-based routing gains track $\alpha$: effective when multiplicity is high (LAMBADA) but counterproductive when low (PIQA). 1.3B, h3-1.}
51	\label{fig:alpha_routing}
52	\end{figure}
53	
54	\paragraph{Confidence margin predictor.} Pure-model confidence on the target token predicts which examples will be affected (AUC 0.70--0.74, a non-parametric measure equivalent to Mann-Whitney $U$; Appendix~\ref{app:confidence_margin}): rescue thresholds are architecture-invariant (1\% spread) but disruption thresholds vary 47\%, explaining cross-architecture Jaccard divergence (Appendix~\ref{app:prediction_framework}). Calibration differences account for less than half the threshold spread (Appendix~\ref{app:calibration}). The rescue pattern generalizes across three benchmarks ($d = 0.13$--$0.60$; Appendix~\ref{app:cm_cross}); disruption is weaker on reasoning tasks (Appendix~\ref{app:boolq}). This

3	Using the model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid} (\S\ref{ssec:model_zoo}), we evaluate all 30 configurations on 10 benchmarks plus supplementary COPA ($n = 100$) at 1.3B scale unless noted. Results at 340M appear in scale analyses. Recall: h$k$-1 denotes one attention layer per $k{+}1$ layers; h3-1 = 25\% attention.
4	
5	\begin{figure}[t]
6	\centering
7	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_per_example_bars.pdf}
8	\caption{Per-example effects of adding attention (pure $\to$ h3-1) on LAMBADA at 1.3B scale. Helped: correct only in the hybrid; hurt: correct only in the pure model. HGRN2 is the only architecture with a net positive effect. Despite comparable magnitudes, these sets overlap minimally across architectures ($\bar{J} \leq 0.10$, all $p < .0001$).}
9	\label{fig:per_example_bars}
10	\end{figure}

12	\begin{table*}[t]
13	\centering
14	\caption{Cross-architecture variance trends across benchmarks (1.3B scale). $\rho$: Spearman correlation between attention ratio and cross-architecture standard deviation $\sigma$ across five configurations. $p$: exact permutation test (two-tailed, 120 permutations). $\sigma$ values reported $\times 10^{2}$ (accuracy percentage points). Ratio: $\sigma_{\text{pure}} / \sigma_{\text{h3-1}}$. \textbf{Bold}: only benchmark with $\rho = -1.0$. HellaSwag evaluated using normalized accuracy (acc\_norm), the standard metric for this benchmark.}

Helped: correct only in the hybrid; hurt: correct only in the pure model. HGRN2 is the only architecture with a net positive effect. Despite comparable magnitudes, these sets overlap minimally across architectures ($\\bar{J} \\leq 0.10$, all $p < .0001$).}\n\\label{fig:per_example_bars}\n\\end{minipage}%\n\\hfill\n\\begin{minipage}[t]{0.48\\textwidth}\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig3_alpha_routing.pdf}\n\\caption{(a) Per-example ambiguity $\\alpha$ varies $2\\times$ across task types.
